 #### Expansive Depth Mode
 
-`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which is set to `maxIterations` when the config is created. Both the mode and the block are locked against the optimizer. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
+`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which is set to `maxIterations` when the config is created. The mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` block is in neither its tunable nor its locked list. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
 
 After the review floor, an expansive run that still holds an open or active node turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`, whichever candidate it was, `all_dimensions_clean` or a composite convergence vote. The veto never creates a stop of its own and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the `minIterations` floor. Under stuck recovery the handler closes the active node as `exhausted` with reason `stuck`, so a node the run cannot fund stops holding the veto open.
 
@@ -171,14 +171,18 @@ Every pivot dispatches three seats, or six when its first attempt misses quorum,
 
 ### Decision Priority
 
-Checks are evaluated in this order (first match wins):
-
-1. **Max iterations** -- hard cap, always checked first.
-2. **All dimensions covered + clean** -- every configured dimension covered (the four by default), no active P0/P1, stabilization passed, gates passed.
-3. **Stuck detection** -- 2+ consecutive no-progress iterations.
-4. **Composite convergence** -- 3-signal weighted vote with threshold 0.60.
-5. **Legal-stop gate bundle** -- review-specific stop gates. If a stop vote fails any gate, persist `stopReason=blockedStop` and continue.
-6. **Default CONTINUE** -- none of the above triggered.
+Checks are evaluated in this order (first match wins), as `buildStopDecision` in `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` runs them:
+
+1. **Max iterations** -- hard cap, always checked first. It outranks every veto below.
+2. **Consecutive failures** -- three trailing failed iterations stop the run with stop reason `error`.
+3. **Stuck detection** -- `stuckThreshold` (2) consecutive no-progress iterations route to stuck recovery.
+4. **Convergence mode off** -- `convergenceMode: off` returns `CONTINUE` (`convergenceModeOff`).
+5. **Stop policy max-iterations** -- `stopPolicy: max-iterations` returns `CONTINUE` (`stopPolicyMaxIterations`).
+6. **Stop candidate** -- all dimensions covered and clean (every configured dimension covered, the four by default, no active P0/P1, coverage stabilized) is a hard candidate; otherwise the 3-signal composite vote with threshold 0.60 is a soft one. No candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`noStopCandidate`).
+7. **Iteration floor** -- below `minIterations` the candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`minIterationsNotReached`).
+8. **Frontier veto** -- under `--depth-mode=expansive`, an open frontier node turns the candidate into `CONTINUE` (`frontierOpen`).
+9. **Legal-stop gate bundle** -- review-specific stop gates. If the candidate fails any gate, persist `stopReason=blockedStop` and continue.
+10. **Graph decision** -- the candidate is combined with the graph convergence verdict: `STOP_ALLOWED` stops, `STOP_BLOCKED` blocks the stop and `CONTINUE` continues the run.

 Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open while a P0 is active or a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.
 
+Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` adds N passes to a finished single-executor run in the same session. A review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults.
+
 A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

 ### Expansive Depth Mode
 
 - `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
-- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, set to `maxIterations` at config creation. Both are locked against the optimizer.
+- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, set to `maxIterations` at config creation. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
 - `step_frontier_seed` writes the depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`, after `step_create_config` or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run. A merge with no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`.
 - `step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, written only by `frontier-admit.cjs`; review has no inbox.
 - `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/feature-catalog/deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md
---
title: "Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)"
description: "The --depth-mode flag on /deep:research and /deep:review selects default or expansive depth, and leaves the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--depth-mode"
  - "depth mode flag"
  - "expansive depth flag"
  - "deep loop depth mode"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no depth field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flag is present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow reads `--depth-mode` and binds it as `depth_mode`. Only the value `expansive` reaches the runtime. `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`, and `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to fan-out lineages only when the value is `expansive`. A default run carries no depth key in either place.

### Packet Behavior

The expansive behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [expansive depth mode for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md) and [expansive depth mode for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md).

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/mode-registry.json` | Registry | Holds the discriminator for `research` and `review`, with no depth field. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row for research. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row for review. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | Adds `depthMode` to the stop-decision config only when expansive. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` | Script | Passes `depthMode` to lineages only when expansive. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/expansive-depth-flag-and-default-identity.md` | Manual playbook | DR-068: the flag defaults to `default` and a default run matches the baseline. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/expansive-depth-flag-and-default-identity.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-076: the review flag defaults to `default` and a default run matches the baseline. |

---

## 4. SOURCE METADATA

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md
---
title: "Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)"
description: "Opt-in --depth-mode=expansive on deep-research seeds depth-one frontier nodes from the topic, admits proposals under caps, and keeps a convergence stop open while frontier nodes stay open."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--depth-mode=expansive"
  - "expansive depth research"
  - "research frontier mode"
  - "frontier admission research"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A run without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` turns on a research frontier: the run seeds depth-one nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as nodes under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps, and holds a convergence stop while frontier nodes stay open. Two live research pilots ran on one topic, and both failed the criterion that a stop candidate forms before the iteration cap: each run stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seeding And Admission

The expansive run seeds depth-one nodes before iteration 1. Each iteration's leaf `frontier_proposal` rows go through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs`, which admits each proposal into the node store. It applies the depth cap, the fan-out cap, the iteration budget, dedupe and cycle checks, and records a reason for each prune.

### Configuration

`expansiveDepth` in `deep-research-config.json` sets `maxDepth` to 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` to 6, and a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths. `antiConvergence.depthMode` defaults to `default`. The convergence script adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`.

### Stop And Next Focus

While a frontier node is open, a composite convergence stop becomes another iteration. Next Focus steers to the shallowest open node. The iteration cap, the anti-convergence floor, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` and the max-iterations policy decide before the frontier veto, so the frontier never extends a run past them.

### Fan-Out

Fan-out lineages each receive a share of the seeded nodes. `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to a lineage only when it is `expansive`. `runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers and runs a fan-in guard over the recorded partition.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Command workflow | The `depth_mode` input, the preflight check and the config keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/deep-research-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the `expansiveDepth` block. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` | Script | Seeds, admits and closes frontier nodes. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | Applies the frontier-open veto and passes `depthMode` into the stop-decision config when expansive. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Builds the opt-in `frontier` registry and the question close rule. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` | Script | Passes `depthMode` to lineages only when expansive. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` | Script | Merges the lineage frontiers and runs the partition guard. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/frontier-admit.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Admission, prune reasons and the close rule for the frontier writer. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/expansive-depth-flag-and-default-identity.md` | Manual playbook | DR-068: the flag defaults to `default` and a default run matches the baseline. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/frontier-open-veto-holds-soft-stop.md` | Manual playbook | DR-072: an open frontier node turns a composite convergence stop into another iteration. |

---

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md
---
title: "Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)"
description: "Opt-in --depth-mode=expansive on deep-review seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target, keeps node status in review/frontier-nodes.jsonl, and holds a review stop while nodes stay open."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--depth-mode=expansive review"
  - "expansive depth review"
  - "review frontier mode"
  - "review frontier-nodes"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A review without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under the same caps as research, keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and holds open nodes as `searchDebt`. One live review pilot ran. Its depth-1 budget check passed, but its stop criterion failed: the root fan-out cap of 6 pruned a seventh depth-1 node, and three of six lineages stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Seeding And Admission

The expansive run seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1. Leaf `frontier_proposal` rows are admitted under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps. Merge commits are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff`, and index rebuilds are pruned as `generated-artifact`.

### Dimension Coverage

The active node's next uncovered dimension is dispatched. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name. Each depth band gets only its `budgetSplit` share of `--max-iterations` (0.5, 0.35 and 0.15), so fund the depth-1 band with at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.

### Stop Behavior

A review stop candidate becomes another iteration while nodes stay open. The `frontierOpen` veto applies to the review stop candidates, including `all_dimensions_clean` and the composite stop, after the minIterations floor.

### Pivot

The divergent review pivot runs only once the frontier is terminal. It seeds from terminal unanswered nodes and routes accepted candidates through admission.

### Fan-Out

Fan-out lineages receive a share of the seeded nodes. `runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` returns the merged review frontier and runs the review fan-in guard over the recorded partition.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | The `--depth-mode` flag row. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | The `depth_mode` input, the frontier seed step and the config keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.depthMode`, the `expansiveDepth` block and the locked `depthMode` field. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` | Script | Admits review proposals as node-store rows and open `searchDebt` entries. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Reads the review frontier, the close rule and the per-node dimension cover. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | Applies the frontier-open veto to review stop candidates. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/scripts/divergent-review-pivot.ts` | Script | Pivots only once the frontier is terminal. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs` | Script | Returns the merged review frontier and runs the fan-in guard. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/frontier-admit-review.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Review admission into the node store and debt entries. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/expansive-depth-flag-and-default-identity.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-076: the flag defaults to `default` and a default run matches the baseline. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/frontier-open-veto-holds-review-stop.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-080: an open frontier node holds a review stop. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/review-pivot-gates-on-terminal-frontier.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-081: the review pivot waits for a terminal frontier. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/fanout/fanout-frontier-partition-round-robin.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-082: a review fan-out deals seeded nodes round-robin. |

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md
---
title: "Completed-run extension (--extend)"
description: "A completed deep-research run extends in place by N iterations under --extend=N, keeps its session identifier and generation, and records one run_extended event."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--extend"
  - "--extend=N"
  - "extend completed research run"
  - "run_extended"
  - "--extend-minutes"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Completed-run extension (--extend)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--extend=N` reopens a completed research run in the same session and generation and adds `N` iterations. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event, and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. `--extend-minutes=M` adds minutes to the duration budget of the same extension.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Lineage And Preconditions

The extension uses lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run must already be complete. An unfinished run continues with `--lineage-mode=resume` instead. The extension is refused with fan-out.

### Iteration And Duration Budget

The iteration cap rises by `N` over the prior count. `--extend-minutes=M` is valid only with `--extend`. The new duration budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs.

### Stop Interaction

Under `stopPolicy: convergence`, an extension with nothing new can stop again at once on `all_questions_answered`. Pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`, or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`.

### Ledger And Reducer Routing

The `run_extended` stem is part of the deep-research ledger schema, and the deep-research reducer routes it. The lifecycle event filters in `deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` read the same event.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | Flag rows for `--extend` and `--extend-minutes`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Command workflow | Lineage handling and the snapshot of the prior synthesis. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-ledger-schema/deep-research-ledger-schema.ts` | Shared | Defines the `run_extended` stem and its payload. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-reducers/deep-research-reducer.ts` | Shared | Routes the `run_extended` stem. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Lifecycle event filters that read the extension event. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-research-reducers.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Reducer routing for the run lifecycle event stems. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-research-run.md` | Manual playbook | DR-065: same-session continuation, the `run_extended` event and the synthesis snapshot. |

---

## 4. SOURCE METADATA

- Group: Loop lifecycle
- Canonical catalog source: `feature-catalog.md`
- Feature file path: `loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md`

Related references:
- [initialization.md](initialization.md) - Initialization and lineage modes
- [synthesis.md](synthesis.md) - Synthesis

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '225,255p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md
Executor settings are owned by the YAML workflow and rendered prompt pack -- never hand-dispatch review iterations; each iteration stays LEAF-only and produces the required markdown plus JSONL delta. Full contract (per-iteration invariants, failure modes, JSONL audit field, config surface/precedence, TrustState surface): `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md`.

### Stop Decision, Counters and Limits

`runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` computes the whole stop decision once per pass from the config, the state log, the findings registry and the coverage graph. `deep-review.yaml` renders no stop arithmetic of its own. The frozen stop reasons are `converged`, `maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `manualStop`, `error`, `stuckRecovery`, `userPaused` and `maxDurationReached`. A missing graph decision blocks every non-terminal STOP.

The stuck count and the consecutive failure count are rebuilt from the state log on every pass, so a resumed run counts the same as one that never paused. Failed iterations stay out of the ratio signals. Three trailing failed iterations stop the loop with stop reason `error`. In confirm mode only, the workflow first asks the operator Retry, Skip or Cancel. Auto mode has no such question.

Each pass also compares `maxDurationMinutes` with the config `createdAt`. When the limit is reached the loop skips the dispatch and goes to synthesis with stop reason `maxDurationReached`.

### Expansive Depth Mode

- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, set to `maxIterations` at config creation. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
- `step_frontier_seed` writes the depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`, after `step_create_config` or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run. A merge with no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`.
- `step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, written only by `frontier-admit.cjs`; review has no inbox.
- `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.
- While a node stays open the frontier veto turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor, and stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted`.
- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size `--max-iterations` so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.

---

## 4. RULES

### ✅ ALWAYS

1. Read JSONL and strategy before review action.
2. Review one dimension per iteration and write findings to `iteration-NNN.md`.
3. Append JSONL with severity counts, finding detail, and `newInfoRatio`.
4. Cite every finding with `[SOURCE: file:line]`. Reject inference-only findings.
5. Re-read cited code before recording any P0.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && for f in .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.*.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.1*.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.1*.md; do echo "===== $f"; sed -n '1,45p' "$f"; done
description: "The research loop protocol now says a file that was dirty before a lineage started and changed during it is reported as contested in fan-out and single-lineage runs, and is never quarantined."
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep-research v1.15.8.0"
  - "deep-research 1.15.8.0"
  - "contested file in research run"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.15.8.0
---

# v1.15.8.0, A File You Were Already Editing Is Reported as Contested

The research loop protocol said to write containment files under the lineage. That covered every new dirty path outside the lineage folder. So a file you were already editing when the run started read as the lineage's own write. The protocol now treats that file as contested. It says where each kind of run reports it.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **A file that already had changes is contested, not the lineage's.** A path was dirty before the lineage started. It changed again during the lineage. So the run makes no quarantine copy, patch or revert. Its pre-dispatch bytes stay in the lineage's `containment/baseline/` folder.
- **Every run tells you about it.** A fan-out run records a `containment_contested` ledger event. It also records a `containmentContested` field on the lineage output. A single-lineage run appends the same event to its state log. The dispatch step prints the paths beside any violations.
- **Everything else is attributed as before.** A path that was clean when the lineage started still counts as the lineage's. Opt-in restore mode is meant for a checkout with one writer. It still puts an already-dirty file back to its pre-dispatch bytes.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Before you trust a contested file, compare it with its copy in `containment/baseline/`. That is because the tree shows that the bytes changed. It does not show who wrote them.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/043-fanout-resume-and-containment` (Level 2)

# v1.15.9.0, A Short Summary Opens the Skill and the Router Keeps Its Own Section

The deep-research skill opened with one long routing section. That section held both a summary and the router itself. A reader had to work through the tables and the discovery code. Only then did they learn what the skill does with a request. The section is now a short prose summary. It says what selects this skill and what loads first. It also says where the rest lives.

The router blocks did not change. The resource domains, the loading levels, the phase signals and the discovery pseudocode moved as they were. They now sit in a final machine router section at the end of the file. Every intent, every resource path and every loading level is the same. So a request routes exactly where it did before.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **The top of the skill reads as a summary.** A few lines say what selects deep-research and what loads first. They also say where the router is. They replace the tables and code.
- **The router keeps its own section.** The resource domains and the loading levels now sit together. So do the phase signals and the discovery pseudocode. They are at the end of the file.
- **Nothing routes differently.** The intent signals, the resource map, the loading levels and the fallback checklist are unchanged.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Anything that pointed at the routing section of this skill now points at the machine router section instead.

title: "deep-research v1.16.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Research"

trigger_phrases:

  - "expansive depth mode"
  - "frontier admission"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.16.0.0
---

# v1.16.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Research

Deep research stops once its starting questions are answered. This release adds an opt-in mode that lets a run grow its own questions.

- Leaves propose new sub-questions while the run works.
- Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the question set.

Runs without the flag keep their behavior.

- Nothing changes unless the flag is passed.
- The one difference is one paragraph in CLI lineage prompts.

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

A run that answers its starting questions narrowly looks finished. Nothing could add a question once the run began. So a broad topic could stop early.

- Narrow coverage looked the same as converged coverage.
- The new mode lets the run widen within fixed bounds.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Widen a run with one flag](#c-widen-a-run-with-one-flag) | One flag turns the mode on for a single run. | Turning It On |
| [Default runs keep their output](#c-default-runs-keep-their-output) | A run without the flag writes what it wrote before, apart from one prompt paragraph. | Turning It On |
| [See the frontier on the dashboard](#c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard) | An expansive run adds a frontier section to its dashboard. | Turning It On |
| [Open questions exist before the first pass](#c-open-questions-exist-before-the-first-pass) | The run has open sub-questions before iteration one. | Admitting New Questions |
| [Every proposal passes six fixed checks](#c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks) | Each proposal meets the same bounds in the same order. | Admitting New Questions |

`deep-review` now makes its review-packet expected behavior explicit across naming, lifecycle, reducer model and runtime mirrors.

## Why This Release

The goal is to make the workflow easier to follow and easier to trust.

---

## What's New at a Glance

- **The review-packet contract is explicit.** It covers artifacts, lifecycle, release-readiness terms and reducer expectations.
- **Runtime and workflow surfaces match the review model.** The runtime mirrors point to one packet model, and the auto and confirm assets carry the workflow expectations.
- **Review guidance follows one lifecycle and state model.** The references, manual testing playbook and config asset reflect the updated expectations.

---

## Review Contract

#### Canonical Artifacts Are Frozen

The canonical review artifacts are `deep-review-config.json`, `deep-review-state.jsonl`, `deep-review-findings-registry.json`, `.deep-review-pause` and `deep-review-dashboard.md`. They are frozen across the skill, references, workflow assets and runtime mirrors.

&nbsp;

#### Review Naming Is Consistent

The main naming is `deep-review-*` across all surfaces. Legacy `deep-research-*` names are limited to the intentional scratch migration path rather than live review surfaces.

&nbsp;

#### Lifecycle and Reducer Expectations

Lifecycle branches are explicit and consistent across the review packet: `resume`, `restart`, `fork` and `completed-continue`. Release-readiness states are first-class expected behavior terms rather than scattered prose. The expected behavior surfaces document reducer inputs, outputs and metrics directly, along with machine-owned boundaries.

The authoritative producer is `step_emit_blocked_stop`. It sits in both `deep_review_{auto,confirm}.yaml` workflows. It emits the 9-gate `gateResults` bundle. This is a documentation-only release. The YAML workflows were read-only sources of truth.

## Why This Release

It closes the gate-model drift cluster LG-0013, LG-0016, LG-0031 and LG-0032. The `003-deep-review` phase-5 deep-research loop surfaced that cluster.

## What's New at a Glance

- **`convergence.md` now documents the full emitted gate set.** The documentation had drifted. It described 6 or 7 gates depending on the surface. It omitted `candidateCoverageGate` and `graphlessFallbackGate`. One table also omitted `claimAdjudicationGate`. The gates, in emission order, are `convergenceGate`, `dimensionCoverageGate`, `p0ResolutionGate`, `evidenceDensityGate`, `hotspotSaturationGate`, `claimAdjudicationGate`, `fixCompletenessReplayGate`, `candidateCoverageGate` and `graphlessFallbackGate`. Its table, `buildReviewLegalStop` pseudocode and recovery-strategy table each add the two missing rows. `scripts/reduce-state.cjs` is gate-name-agnostic and was confirmed unchanged.
- **`loop_protocol.md` now describes all nine gates.** §Step-2 corrects its former seven-gate description. It identifies `candidateCoverageGate` and `graphlessFallbackGate` as v2-rollout gates.
- **`state_format.md` updates its event example.** The `blocked_stop` example grows from five gates to the full nine-gate `gateResults` bundle. It points to `convergence.md` §Section-1 as the authoritative shape.
- **`feature_catalog` now enumerates its quality gates.** `04--severity-system/quality-gates.md` §2 CURRENT REALITY has a table of all nine gate names. Each name has a one-line check description. It explains how the three contract-level binary gates relate to the nine named legal-stop bundle gates.
- **`DRV-018` connects its checks to the gate bundle.** The scenario validates the three contract-level binary gates for evidence, scope and coverage. Its WHY THIS MATTERS section maps those checks to the nine named event-shape gates. So the three-gate scenario is not in tension with the model. The stale `convergence.md` Section 10.4 anchor now points to §6 LEGAL-STOP GATE BUNDLE.

No migration required.

# v1.10.1.0: Reference And Asset Alignment

This release aligns `deep-review` with the shared deep-skill reference model. It does this through focused convergence and state-output references. It moves the version from `1.10.0.0` to `1.10.1.0`. Loop behavior is unchanged. `/deep:review` still owns state, dispatch, reducer updates, convergence checks and synthesis. No runtime YAML, reducer, script, command or agent behavior changed.

## What's New at a Glance

- **The focused references follow the deep-skill shape.** They do not import research or council vocabulary. `references/convergence.md` and `references/state-format.md` remain hubs. Narrower files handle operator navigation.
- **Reference topics now have dedicated pages.** `references/convergence-signals.md` covers severity-weighted STOP signals, P0 override, legal stop and sibling threshold contrast. `references/state-outputs.md` covers packet outputs, iteration markdown, dashboard, final report and resource-map behavior. `references/state-reducer-registry.md` covers reducer ownership, registry semantics, fail-closed behavior and reconstruction.
- **The focused references are loaded for named review flows.** `SKILL.md` routing loads them for setup, iteration, convergence, synthesis, stuck and on-demand flows. README structure and related documents list the focused references.
- **No new review assets were required.** Existing config, strategy, dashboard, prompt-pack, review contract and runtime capability assets remain canonical.

## Upgrade

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.11.1.0.md
---
title: "deep-review v1.11.1.0"
description: "A finished fan-out review now closes as complete and reports its real iteration count, and review confirm runs to its ceiling under --stop-policy=max-iterations."
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep-review v1.11.1.0"
  - "deep-review 1.11.1.0"
  - "fan-out review closes complete"
  - "review stop policy honored"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.11.1.0
---

A fan-out review splits one review across several models. Each model runs its own lineage. Such a review now closes as complete once its report is written. It also counts its iterations correctly. Review confirm keeps going to its ceiling when asked to.

> Part of [Skilled v4.0.0.2](../../../../changelog/skilled/v4.0.0.2.md).
## What's New at a Glance

- **A finished fan-out review closes as complete.** The close-out required a dashboard at the review root. A fan-out review never writes one, because each lineage keeps its own. So every finished fan-out review was logged as incomplete. The root dashboard is now required only when no lineage logs exist. A single review without one still fails.
- **A fan-out review reports its real iteration count.** Review now folds its lineage state logs into its totals, the way research does.
- **`--stop-policy=max-iterations` holds in review confirm.** The confirm workflow now runs to its iteration ceiling when asked. Before, it stopped at convergence.
- **The close-out runs from one shared script.** Both review workflows call the deep-loop runtime's close-out script. That script refuses a symlinked artifact.
- **The skill says where fan-out dashboards live.** The completion rule in `SKILL.md` says a fan-out run keeps its dashboards per lineage.

## Upgrade

No migration required.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-skill-advisor/030-pi-skill-orchestrator-based-research-refinement` (Level 2)
===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.11.10.0.md
---
title: "deep-review v1.11.10.0, a file you were already editing is reported as contested"
description: "The review loop protocol now says a file that was dirty before a lineage started and changed during it is reported as contested in fan-out and single-lineage runs, and is never quarantined."
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep-review v1.11.10.0"
  - "deep-review 1.11.10.0"
  - "contested file in review run"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.11.10.0
---

# v1.11.10.0, A File You Were Already Editing Is Reported as Contested

The review loop protocol said to write containment files. It said to do this for every new dirty path outside the lineage folder. It said to write them under the lineage. So a file you were already editing read as the reviewer's own write. That happened while a review ran. The protocol now treats that file as contested. It also says where each kind of run reports it.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **A file that already had changes is contested, not the lineage's.** A path was dirty before the lineage started. If it changed again during the run, the run makes no quarantine copy, patch or revert. Its pre-dispatch bytes stay in the lineage's `containment/baseline/` folder.
- **Every run tells you about it.** A fan-out run records a `containment_contested` ledger event. It also records a `containmentContested` field on the lineage output. A single-lineage run appends the same event to its state log. The dispatch step prints the paths beside any violations.
- **Everything else is attributed as before.** A path that was clean when the lineage started still counts as the lineage's. A lane that produced its review artifacts still settles as complete. Opt-in restore mode still puts an already-dirty file back to its pre-dispatch bytes. That mode is meant for a checkout with one writer.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Before you trust a contested file, compare it with its copy in `containment/baseline/`. The tree shows that the bytes changed. It does not show who wrote them.

&nbsp;

# v1.11.16.0, One Workflow File and a Stop a Script Computes

A review run now stops for a reason a script computed from files. Auto mode and confirm mode run the same steps from one workflow file. The docs in this packet named workflow files and a reducer path that do not exist. They now match the code.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **One workflow file serves both modes.** `/deep:review` runs from `deep-review.yaml`. The files `deep-review-auto.yaml` and `deep-review-confirm.yaml` are gone. Confirm mode adds approval gates and nothing else.
- **A script computes the stop decision.** `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` runs the nine legal-stop gates and decides once per pass. `maxDurationReached` joins the stop reasons.
- **A resumed run counts the same.** The stuck count and the failure count are rebuilt from the state log on every pass.
- **Three failed iterations in a row stop the loop.** The stop reason is `error`. Confirm mode first asks Retry, Skip or Cancel. Auto mode asks nothing.
- **`--restart`, `--lineage-mode` and `--dry-run` work in both modes.** A dry run halts before any dispatch or state write.
- **The docs name the real reducer.** `SKILL.md` and the state references said `scripts/reduce-state.cjs`. The review reducer is `runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs`.
- **The docs state no tool-call number.** `SKILL.md` and `references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` point at the `- Target` line of `assets/prompt-pack-iteration.md.tmpl`.
- **The contract snapshot names its source.** `scripts/render-contract-snapshot.cjs` said `review_mode_contract.yaml`. The file is `review-mode-contract.yaml`, and `assets/review-mode-contract-snapshot.md` was regenerated.
- **The docs name files that exist.** `SKILL.md`, seven files under `references/` and the files under `feature-catalog/` and `manual-testing-playbook/` were corrected. The native fan-out scenario now describes `fanout-run.cjs`.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

Replace `deep-review-auto.yaml` or `deep-review-confirm.yaml` in your own scripts with `deep-review.yaml`. Nothing else is required.

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.11.8.0.md
---
title: "deep-review v1.11.8.0, severity follows the reach calibration"
description: "The deep-review severity table now defers to the reach calibration its review reference applies, so a finding that needs input the code rules out drops to P2 hardening instead of blocking."
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep-review v1.11.8.0"
  - "deep-review 1.11.8.0"
  - "deep review severity calibration"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.11.8.0
---

# v1.11.8.0, Severity Follows the Reach Calibration

A deep review could call a missing check a blocker on a helper. That helper never receives the value it worried about. The severity table now says a P0 or P1 finding must name input the code's contract allows. The other option is a real caller that sends it. So the loop's own scale no longer contradicts the calibration. Its review reference already applies that calibration.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **A blocker has to name a reachable input.** "Missing validation" is P1 only when the unvalidated input can arrive. Validation the code never claimed is P2 hardening. That is for input outside its stated contract.
- **A trust boundary stays wide.** Every value the outside can send is in reach there. So security findings keep the severity they had.
- **The loop decides the way a single review does.** The paragraph points at the calibration in the review reference. So deep review and a one-pass review rate the same failing input the same way.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/sk-code/016-review-mode-followups` (Level 2)

# v1.11.9.0, The Agent Finds Its Skills Where They Are Installed

The deep-review agent pointed at fixed `.skilled/` paths. It pointed at them for its review doctrine. It also pointed at them for its event gateway, its reducer and its retrieval helpers. So a repository that installed the skills in another folder sent it to missing files. The agent now names each file by its place. That place is inside the installed skill. The code agent already does this.

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/changelog/v1.13.0.0.md
---
title: "deep-review v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review"
description: "Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. An opt-in expansive depth mode admits, steers toward and closes them. Runs without the flag keep their output."
trigger_phrases:
  - "deep-review v1.13.0.0"
  - "deep-review 1.13.0.0"
  - "expansive depth mode"
  - "review frontier"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 1.13.0.0
---

# v1.13.0.0, Expansive Depth Mode for Review

Deep review can now split its target into sub-targets and work through them. This release adds an opt-in expansive depth mode for that.

- Reviews propose sub-targets while they work.
- Each proposal passes fixed checks before it joins the frontier.

Runs without the flag keep their behavior.

- Nothing changes unless the flag is passed.
- The one difference is one paragraph in CLI lineage prompts.

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

A review that checks its target in one pass can look finished too early. Nothing could split a sub-target out once the review began. So a large target could stop before each part got its own look.

- A narrow pass looked the same as a thorough one.
- The new mode lets the review widen within fixed bounds.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

| Change | What It Means | Section |
|---|---|---|
| [Widen a review with one flag](#c-widen-a-review-with-one-flag) | One flag turns the mode on for a single run. | Turning It On |
| [Default runs keep their output](#c-default-runs-keep-their-output) | A run without the flag writes what it wrote before, apart from one prompt paragraph. | Turning It On |
| [See the frontier on the dashboard](#c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard) | An expansive run adds a frontier section to its dashboard. | Turning It On |
| [Sub-targets exist before the first pass](#c-start-from-depth-one-sub-targets) | The review splits its target before iteration one. | Admitting Sub-Targets |
| [Every proposal passes six fixed checks](#c-every-proposal-passes-six-fixed-checks) | Each proposal meets the same bounds in the same order. | Admitting Sub-Targets |

===== .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/changelog/v2.1.2.0.md
---
title: "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0, merged fan-out reviews keep their coverage"
description: "A merged two-lineage review fan-out keeps the coverage its lineages recorded: dimension, traceability, search, candidate, divergence and ruled-out surfaces are carried through the merge."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system-deep-loop v2.1.2.0"
  - "system-deep-loop 2.1.2.0"
  - "fan-out review coverage merge"
  - "divergenceByLineage"
importance_tier: "normal"
contextType: "general"
version: 2.1.2.0
---
# v2.1.2.0, Merged Fan-Out Reviews Keep Their Coverage

A review that fans out to several lineages used to lose the coverage each lineage had recorded when the results were merged. The merge now carries those surfaces through, so the merged review shows what every lineage covered.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- **Dimension coverage is a union.** `dimensionCoverage` holds the dimensions every lineage covered, with sorted keys.
- **Traceability keeps the strictest status.** Per protocol, `fail` beats `blocked`, `blocked` beats `partial`, `partial` beats `pass`, and `pass` beats `notApplicable`. An undecided status never replaces a decided one.
- **Search and candidate coverage are merged.** `searchCoverage` holds sorted unions. Each `candidateCoverage` bucket lists the lineages that reported it.
- **Divergence stays per lineage.** `divergenceByLineage` keeps each lineage's pivot state under its label. No merged `divergence` is written, because a merged current focus would be invented.
- **Ruled-out candidates name their lineage.** Each entry carries a `lineage` field and is deduplicated on lineage and id.
- **The merge is order-independent.** Lineages are read in label order, so the same lineages give the same merged result whatever order they arrive in.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. This release adds the data only. No stop decision reads these surfaces yet.

&nbsp;

> Spec folder: `specs/system-deep-loop/loop-architecture/002-graph-engineering/007-expansive-depth-mode/009-fanout-review-coverage-merge`

# v2.1.3.0, Free Text No Longer Steers Next Focus

Next Focus could follow a sentence that only reported progress. A run then spent a round on a settled question. Next Focus now follows open questions and the latest leaf's recommendation.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- Research carries forward only open questions. Status sentences and answered questions no longer return.
- Research reads the latest leaf's `Recommended Next Focus`. It leads while a machine question is open, unless that focus is already answered or rejected.
- Review names the first uncovered dimension. Leaf free text that names no dimension is ignored.
- Imperative items, such as `Investigate the review twin`, no longer carry forward. The leaf's recommendation can still carry one into Next Focus.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

Seven new cases failed before the change and pass after it. The three reducer and continuity suites pass, and every existing case is kept.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Normal research and review runs use the new focus rules with no flag.

&nbsp;

# v2.1.4.0, Divergent Pivots Get One Quorum Retry

A divergent pivot ended on its first quorum miss. One missed seat could end the pivot. A pivot now gets one retry before it stops.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` now means three per council attempt.
- A first-attempt quorum miss gets one retry.
- A second miss still ends the pivot. No third attempt opens.
- A later pivot is no longer refused because earlier pivots used seats.
- A run uses at most 18 seat outputs. That is three pivots with six seats each.
- Research and review pivots follow the same rule.

&nbsp;

Eleven new cases cover the seat budget, the retry and the attempt identity. The divergent pivot suites pass. Attempt 1 keeps its existing paths and event ids.

&nbsp;

# v2.1.5.0, Landed Iterations Are Not Dispatched Twice

- Those failures appear only after the iteration's narrative, state record and delta exist.
- Research and review follow the same rule.
- Every other failure still gets one redispatch, including route proof.

&nbsp;

Two checks could not see the fields a leaf wrote. Both now read them. They run on every research run, with no flag.

&nbsp;

## What's New at a Glance

- The state log keeps ten leaf fields.
- The list includes `findingsCount`, `keyQuestions` and `sourcesQueried`.
- The `findings_not_enumerated` gate reads the real `findingsCount`.
- `focusAlignment` is live on default research runs.
- It judges the latest record that carries both question lists.
- Reducer consumers now receive the fields.
- A single `keyQuestions` entry resolves its question.
- `sourcesQueried` feeds the source list.
- The dashboard shows `focusTrack` and `findingsCount`.

&nbsp;

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

A `focusAlignment` failure can now hold a run at CONTINUE. The hold clears on the next aligned iteration. The guard judges only the latest record with both lists. The prompt pack asks for the exact question text. Write `keyQuestions` with no id prefix, such as `Q1:`.

&nbsp;

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && cat .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/frontier-admission-prunes-with-reasons.md
---
title: "DR-070 -- Frontier admission prunes each proposal with its reason"

# DR-070 -- Frontier admission prunes each proposal with its reason

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-070`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the admission step that turns a leaf's `frontier_proposal` rows into questions. The objective is to confirm that a proposal failing a check is pruned with the reason of the first failing check, and that only an admitted proposal writes an inbox row.

### Why This Matters

Leaves over-propose. Admission is the gate that keeps the question set bounded, so each rejection must name its cause. An operator who reads a pruned row must be able to tell which bound it hit.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the six reasons, the prune cases and the admit case.

- Objective: Confirm that each failing check prunes with its own reason, that the first failing check wins and that a pruned proposal never reaches the inbox.
- Real user request: Show me why a proposed sub-question was rejected, and show me that a valid one was added.
- Prompt: `Check that every frontier proposal that fails an admission check is pruned with that check's reason, and that only the admitted proposal reaches the research inbox with origin frontier. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Read the reason returns in the admission helper, then run the prune rows, the admit case and the pruned-record case by file stem.
- Expected signals: The helper returns the six reasons, `relevance`, `dedupe`, `depth`, `fanout`, `budget` and `cycle`. The seven prune rows pass. The admitted proposal writes exactly one inbox row with `origin: "frontier"`. The pruned proposal writes a store row and no inbox row.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can name the bound each rejected proposal hit, and can see the one proposal that entered the question set.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the six reasons appear, all seven prune rows and both record cases pass; FAIL if a proposal is pruned with another check's reason, or a pruned proposal reaches the inbox.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that every frontier proposal that fails an admission check is pruned with that check's reason, and that only the admitted proposal reaches the research inbox with origin frontier. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n "return '(relevance|dedupe|depth|fanout|budget|cycle)'" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "prunes with" --reporter=verbose`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "admits a valid proposal" --reporter=verbose`
4. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "records a pruned proposal" --reporter=verbose`

### Expected

Command 1 prints 14 lines. Every one of the six reason strings appears, because some reasons are returned from more than one guard. Command 2 prints `Tests  7 passed | 17 skipped (24)`, with seven prune rows: two for `relevance`, and one each for `dedupe`, `depth`, `fanout`, `budget` and `cycle`. Command 3 prints `Tests  1 passed | 23 skipped (24)`, and that case asserts one inbox row with `origin: "frontier"` and one open store row. Command 4 prints `Tests  1 passed | 23 skipped (24)`, and that case asserts a store row with no inbox line.

### Evidence

- The `rg` lines from command 1 and the verbose case lines from commands 2 to 4.
- The summary line and exit status for each command.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: All six reasons appear, every command exits 0 with the expected pass count and no failed count.
- **Fail**: A proposal is pruned with another check's reason, a pruned proposal reaches the inbox, or any command fails.

### Failure Triage

- A proposal is pruned with the wrong reason: check the order of the checks in the admission helper, since the first failing check must win.
- A pruned proposal reaches the inbox: check that the inbox write follows only an admitted result.
- A reason string is missing from command 1: a check no longer returns its reason, so restore the return before trusting the verdict.

---

## 4. SOURCE FILES

### Playbook Sources

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `manual-testing-playbook.md` | Root directory page and scenario summary |

### Implementation And Test Anchors

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` | Six ordered checks in `checkProposal`, the admit path and the store row for each outcome |
| `frontier-admit.vitest` | One row per failing check, first failure wins, one admitted row and one pruned row |

---

- Group: ITERATION EXECUTION AND STATE DISCIPLINE

- Feature catalog status: `feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md` covers the expansive admission step in its Seeding And Admission section.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '88,100p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/SKILL.md && echo '---' && sed -n '234,245p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md
### Expansive Depth Mode

- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions beside the seeded key questions. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior. The one byte change is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt, which the golden test strips before it compares.
- `step_frontier_seed` writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`, after `step_create_config`, or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run.
- `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Admitted nodes land in `research/inbox.jsonl` with `origin: "frontier"`.
- Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first, then the least visited, then the oldest, and writes `Frontier node <id> (depth N, scope S): <question>`.
- After the `minIterations` floor an open or active node turns a soft stop into CONTINUE with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never creates a stop and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`.
- A fan-out run deals the seeded depth-1 nodes round-robin across lineages and merges them back under a partition guard, so no dealt node goes unreported.

### Invocation Contract

This skill is invoked exclusively through `/deep:research:auto` or `/deep:research:confirm` -- the command YAML owns state, dispatch, convergence, and synthesis. Never simulate the loop with ad hoc shell dispatch, nested CLI loops, direct `@deep-research` Task dispatch, `/tmp` prompt files, or state outside the resolved local research packet.
---

### Expansive Depth Mode

- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, set to `maxIterations` at config creation. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
- `step_frontier_seed` writes the depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`, after `step_create_config` or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run. A merge with no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`.
- `step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, written only by `frontier-admit.cjs`; review has no inbox.
- `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.
- While a node stays open the frontier veto turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor, and stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted`.
- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size `--max-iterations` so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.

---
description: "Autonomous deep-review loop: iterative code audit with convergence detection. Modes :auto, :confirm."
argument-hint: "<target> [:auto|:confirm] [--spec-folder=PATH] [--max-iterations=N] [--executor=TYPE] [--concurrency=N] [--dry-run] [--restart] [--extend=N]"
allowed-tools: Read, Write, Edit, Bash, Grep, Glob, Task
---

# Deep Start Review Loop

Thin router for the deep-review loop. This command verifies the orchestrating agent, resolves setup and execution mode, loads the presentation contract, then executes the owned workflow YAML.

### MANDATORY INPUT GATE

- **DO NOT** dispatch any agent from this document.
- **ALL** agent dispatching is handled by the YAML workflow steps.
- **MARKDOWN OWNS SETUP**: resolve setup inputs here first, then hand off to YAML.
- **YAML START CONDITION**: do not load YAML until ALL required inputs are bound:
  - `review_target`, `review_target_type`, `review_dimensions`, `spec_folder`, `execution_mode`, `maxIterations`, `convergenceThreshold`, `convergence_mode`, `depth_mode`, `stop_policy`, `lineage_mode`
  - `dry_run` (default false unless `--dry-run` is present)

## 1. ROUTER CONTRACT

Do not dispatch agents from this Markdown file. Agent dispatch, workflow steps, and artifact-writing behavior are owned by the workflow YAML assets.

Load the presentation contract before showing startup questions, dashboards, checkpoints, success output, failure output, examples, or next-step prompts.


> **EXECUTION PROTOCOL -- READ FIRST**
>
> This command runs a structured YAML workflow. Do NOT dispatch agents from this document.
>
> **YOUR FIRST ACTION (HARD-BLOCK gate — do not skip):**
> 1. Run the Unified Setup Phase (BLOCKED gate) through the presentation contract and resolve:
>    - `review_target`
>    - `review_target_type`
>    - `review_dimensions`
>    - `spec_folder`
>    - `execution_mode`
>    - `maxIterations`
>    - `convergenceThreshold`
>    - `convergence_mode` (default `default` unless `--convergence-mode` is present)
>    - `depth_mode` (default `default` unless `--depth-mode=expansive` is present)
>    - `stop_policy` (default `convergence` unless `--stop-policy=max-iterations` is present)
>    - `lineage_mode` (default `auto` unless `--lineage-mode` or `--restart` is present)
>    - `dry_run` (default false unless `--dry-run` is present)
> 2. Load the workflow YAML from `assets/` only after all setup values are resolved: `deep-review.yaml`. The mode binds `execution_mode` to `auto` or `confirm`.
> 3. Execute the YAML workflow step by step using those resolved values
>
> This command is **general-agent based** — it orchestrates the deep-review loop. The Unified Setup Phase (the BLOCKED gate) is a HARD BLOCK; it may not be skipped.

This command is **general-agent based**. Do not use the Task tool to spawn the general agent; the default primary agent is the command orchestrator when it has the tools listed below. Keep raw agent handles out of this command body because OpenCode parses them as delegation requests.

In `:auto` mode, an explicit `--restart` or `--lineage-mode=restart` flag (invocation form `--restart|--lineage-mode=restart`) is operator authorization to archive the resolved review packet before fan-out or phase init. Do not ask for a second confirmation; preserve rollback by moving the timestamped archive directory back to `review/` if needed.

### AUTONOMOUS EXECUTION DIRECTIVE (:auto)

> Read this first. It is an imperative to you, the executor — not background information.

You were invoked with the `:auto` suffix and a bound spec_folder and review target in your message. Setup is already resolved.

- DO NOT emit the consolidated setup question (target/mode, review target, Spec Folder A/B/C/D, execution mode, max iterations, executor). The bound spec_folder is your write authority; do not ask which spec folder should own the writes.
- DO NOT halt for the repo-wide `CLAUDE.md` Gate-3 documentation gate. Under AUTONOMOUS execution with a validated prebound spec folder, Gate 3 is already satisfied by that folder (the prebound spec folder is your write authority).
- PROCEED immediately: load the workflow YAML with `execution_mode` bound to `auto` and execute it step by step.
- THE WORKFLOW DISPATCHES: the workflow YAML owns setup, the dispatch of the `deep-review` agent for each iteration, reducer sync, convergence checks, synthesis and all loop-level artifact writes. You do not dispatch from this document, and you do not read, edit, patch or run the loop over the target yourself. The `deep-review` leaf owns only its own single-iteration artifacts.
- ROUTE PROOF: dispatch through the workflow with `execution_mode` bound to `auto` and its prompt pack so `deep-review` writes each iteration state record with the route-proof fields present — `target_agent: "deep-review"`, `resolved_route`, `agent_definition_loaded: true`, and `mode: "review"`. A completed run whose iteration state records omit these fields is an incomplete delegation and does not pass.

This contract is your instruction set. The review target is the bound spec_folder or target named in your message, never this document.

---

## 2. OWNED ASSETS

| Purpose | Asset |
|---------|-------|
| Presentation source of truth | `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review-presentation.txt` |
| Workflow | `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` (`execution_mode` binds to `auto` or `confirm`) |

---

## 3. MODE ROUTING

1. Parse `$ARGUMENTS` for attached command suffixes (`:auto` or `:confirm`). Canonical mode syntax is `/deep:review:auto` and `/deep:review:confirm`.
2. Treat the target text, pre-bound setup answers and every flag in the Workflow Flag Surface table below as workflow inputs, not execution modes. The table is the only list of flags.
3. If `:auto` is present, set `execution_mode = AUTONOMOUS` and resolve required setup inputs through the presentation contract's three-tier auto setup resolution before loading YAML.
4. If `:confirm` is present, set `execution_mode = INTERACTIVE` and use the presentation contract's consolidated setup prompt before loading YAML.
5. If no mode suffix is present, set `execution_mode = ASK` and use the presentation contract's consolidated setup prompt to ask for execution mode.
6. Load the selected workflow asset only after `review_target`, `review_target_type`, `review_dimensions`, `spec_folder`, `execution_mode`, `maxIterations`, `convergenceThreshold`, `convergence_mode`, `depth_mode`, `stop_policy`, `lineage_mode` and `dry_run` are bound.

### Workflow Flag Surface

The argument hint names the invocation shape; the whole flag surface is here. Every flag below
is a workflow input, never an execution mode, and the presentation asset owns how each one binds
into `config`.

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default. `divergent` routes an eligible convergence stop into a Council pivot rather than ending the run. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (record the pilot range SHA; merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, and turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the resource-map write; the review report still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--review-focus` | `"TEXT"` | Optional operator focus that every iteration covers in addition to its dimension, in single-executor and fan-out runs alike. It is cut to one line of at most 1000 characters, stored as `reviewFocus` in the config and shown in the review report. |
| `--restart`, `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing; `restart`, which bare `--restart` selects, opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `review/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior review report as `review/review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_dimensions_clean` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off` or use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`. |
| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
| `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Each occurrence opens a group accepting `--model=X`, `--config-dir=PATH`, `--reasoning-effort=LEVEL`, `--service-tier=TIER`, `--executor-timeout=SECONDS`, `--iters=N`, `--count=N` and `--label=X`. Which of those a given kind supports is in the presentation asset. |
| `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

Fan-out policy: zero or one `--executor` runs the single-executor path, and so does an
`--executors` matrix that expands to exactly one lineage. Its `iterations` value binds to
`config.maxIterations`, and its executor settings populate `config.executor`. Two or more
`--executor` flags, a matrix expanding to two or more lineages, or any `--count` above 1
selects fan-out. A direct call of the fan-out runner that expands to one research or review
lineage is refused and names this workflow instead, because the runner alone skips packet
init, synthesis and save. `--fanout-lineage-artifact-dir` is internal and
set by the runtime, not by a caller. Under `:auto` a `PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:` block in the
prompt body binds the same values without an interactive prompt.

### Lineage Timeout Flag

`--lineage-timeout-hours <N>` may only narrow the per-lineage wall-clock timeout ceiling below the 4-hour hard maximum; a value above 4 is rejected outright. Full-lineage lifetime is non-disableable -- omit the flag to keep the 4h ceiling, or pass a lower `<N>` to cap a lineage more tightly.

### Stop Policy Flag

`--stop-policy <convergence|max-iterations>` (invocation form `--stop-policy=convergence|max-iterations`) selects whether convergence may stop the loop early.
The default `convergence` stops at legal convergence or `config.maxIterations`, whichever comes first.
`max-iterations` treats convergence as telemetry only and forces the loop to continue until `config.maxIterations`, which is useful for forced-depth runs.

### Dry-Run Flag

`--dry-run` is a first-class flag in both execution modes, not a third execution mode. It still performs real setup resolution, artifact-root resolution, focus selection, prompt rendering and convergence reads when those steps can run without side effects.

When `dry_run=true`, the workflow emits `dry_run_halt` JSONL preview lines to terminal output instead of appending to the live state log, and halts before executor dispatch, persistent state mutation, reducer refresh or child-lineage spawn.
Under dry run the setup phase binds its values in memory and does not persist `{artifact_dir}/deep-review-config.json`.
A fresh packet and an existing packet both stop at the directory creation step, which is the first persistent state boundary.

| `:auto` or `:confirm` | `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml`, with `execution_mode` bound to `auto` or `confirm` |

## 5. PRESENTATION BOUNDARY

The following content lives only in `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review-presentation.txt`:

- Startup-question wording, consolidated setup prompt text, question text, and reply-format examples.
- `:auto` pre-bound setup answer schema, default resolution table, targeted-ask rules, and fail-fast display behavior.
- Dashboard/checkpoint layouts, workflow overview displays, mode behavior descriptions, and review reference displays.
- Success and failure result templates, error-handling displays, and verdict wording.
- Example invocations, fan-out explanation display, and next-step suggestion wording.
