## 3. MODE ROUTING

1. Parse `$ARGUMENTS` for attached suffixes: `:auto` sets `execution_mode = AUTONOMOUS`; `:confirm` sets `execution_mode = INTERACTIVE`; no suffix sets `execution_mode = ASK`.
2. Treat every flag in the Workflow Flag Surface table below as a workflow input, not an execution mode. The table is the only list of flags.
3. For `:auto`, resolve setup from `$ARGUMENTS` flags, any `PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:` marker block, scope-extracted spec-folder paths, and the presentation contract's default resolution table. When all required fields are resolved, persist `{artifact_dir}/deep-research-config.json`, bind runtime YAML placeholders, and load `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` with `execution_mode` bound to `auto`.
4. In `:auto`, ask a targeted Tier-2 question only for `spec_folder` when the topic is present, names no resolvable spec folder, and the folder choice is ambiguous. Missing `research_topic` is absence, not ambiguity; use the named-missing-inputs fail-fast format from the auto-mode contract and do not load YAML.
5. For `:confirm`, use the presentation contract's consolidated setup prompt to bind missing setup values, then load `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` with `execution_mode` bound to `confirm`.
6. For no suffix, use the presentation contract's consolidated setup prompt to choose execution mode and bind missing setup values, then load `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` with `execution_mode` bound to the chosen mode.
7. Lightweight read-only discovery for related spec folders or prior memory may support setup, but it must feed the single consolidated prompt and never split setup questions.
8. After the selected workflow asset is loaded, execute it step by step using the resolved setup values.

### Workflow Flag Surface

The argument hint names the invocation shape; the whole flag surface is here. Every flag below
is a workflow input, never an execution mode, and the presentation asset owns how each one binds
into `config`.

| Flag | Value | Effect |
|------|-------|--------|
| `--spec-folder` | `PATH` | Binds the packet that owns this run's writes. |
| `--max-iterations` | `N` | Hard ceiling on loop iterations. |
| `--convergence` | `N` | Threshold the loop must clear before convergence may stop it. |
| `--convergence-mode` | `default` \| `off` \| `sliding-window` \| `divergent` | `default` keeps the anti-convergence `minIterations` floor. `off` disables convergence-driven STOP while max-iterations, pause and halt stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window, five snapshots by default, on the ordinary legal-stop path. `divergent` sends a `composite_converged` or `all_questions_answered` stop into a one-round three-seat Council pivot instead of ending the run, at proportionally higher cost. |
| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open. It never outranks the iteration cap, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
| `--lineage-timeout-hours` | `N` | See Lineage Timeout Flag below. |
| `--stop-policy` | `convergence` \| `max-iterations` | See Stop Policy Flag below. |
| `--no-resource-map` | no value | Suppresses the `research/resource-map.md` write; `research.md` still emits. |
| `--dry-run` | no value | See Dry-Run Flag below. |
| `--restart` | no value | Shorthand that selects `restart`: opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. |
| `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing. `restart` opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. The run must be complete, so use `--lineage-mode=resume` for an unfinished one. Refused with fan-out. Under `stopPolicy: convergence` an extension with nothing new can re-stop on `all_questions_answered` at once: pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations` or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`. |
| `--extend-minutes` | positive integer, as `--extend-minutes=M` | Minutes added to the duration budget on an extension, default the prior `maxDurationMinutes`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
| `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Each occurrence opens a group accepting `--model=X`, `--config-dir=PATH`, `--reasoning-effort=LEVEL`, `--service-tier=TIER`, `--executor-timeout=SECONDS`, `--iters=N`, `--count=N` and `--label=X`. Which of those a given kind supports, and the fail-fast on an unsupported pairing, are in the presentation asset. |
| `--executors` | `JSON` | Declares the executor matrix in one value; one expanded lineage selects `config.executor`, while two or more select `config.fanout`. |
| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

Fan-out policy: zero or one `--executor` runs the single-executor path, and so does an
`--executors` matrix that expands to exactly one lineage. Its `iterations` value binds to
`config.maxIterations`, and its executor settings populate `config.executor`. Two or more
`--executor` flags, a matrix expanding to two or more lineages, or any `--count` above 1
selects fan-out. A direct call of the fan-out runner that expands to one research or review
lineage is refused and names this workflow instead, because the runner alone skips packet
init, synthesis and save. Under `:auto` a `PRE-BOUND SETUP ANSWERS:` block
in the prompt body binds the same values without an interactive prompt.

### Lineage Timeout Flag

`--lineage-timeout-hours <N>` optionally narrows the per-lineage wall-clock timeout ceiling below the default 4 hours; values above the hard 4-hour maximum are rejected, and omitting the flag keeps the 4h default.

### Stop Policy Flag

`--stop-policy <convergence|max-iterations>` selects whether convergence may stop the loop early. The default `convergence` stops at legal convergence or `config.maxIterations`, whichever comes first; `max-iterations` treats convergence as telemetry only and forces the loop to continue until `config.maxIterations`, which is useful for forced-depth runs.

### Dry-Run Flag

`--dry-run` is a first-class flag in both execution modes, not a third execution mode. It still performs real setup resolution, artifact-root resolution, focus selection, prompt rendering, and convergence reads when those steps can run without side effects.

          {"pruneReason":"generated-artifact"}. Write them as a JSON array to
          {artifact_dir}/frontier-seed.json, one object per sub-target:
          {"question": "what to review in it", "scope": "<paths inside the
          review target>", "evidence": "<path or commit that grounds it>"}. Then
          run the command. frontier-admit.cjs is the only writer of the node
          store; never write that file by hand, and never queue or hand-expand
          the admitted nodes yourself.
        command: 'node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review --artifact-dir "{artifact_dir}" --iteration 0 --input "{artifact_dir}/frontier-seed.json"'
        working_directory: "{repo_root}"
        outputs:
          - frontier_seed_json: "The JSON line the command prints: admitted node ids and pruned {nodeId, reason} rows"
        on_failure: "Halt with the command's stderr line. A run that asked for expansive depth must not start without its seeded frontier."

      step_create_state_log:
        action: "Open the run in the ledger: record deep_review.run_initialized through the append gateway, which writes the state log's first row"
        dry_run_boundary: "state-mutation"
        note: |
          The state log is a projection the gateway rebuilds from the ledger, so this step never writes
          it directly. A config row written beside the ledger carries keys the projection cannot
          rebuild, and the projection refuses to drop them, so every later append in the run would
          commit to the ledger and then exit 2. Recording the run's opening as a ledger event makes
          the projected config row the state log's first row from the start. The full config stays
          in deep-review-config.json, which is what the reducer reads.
        command: |
          GATEWAY=".skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/append-mode-event.cjs"
          [ -f "$GATEWAY" ] || GATEWAY="scripts/append-mode-event.cjs"

---
title: "Initialization"
description: "Sets up a research packet and locks the lineage contract before the first iteration runs."

  - "initialization"
  - "packet setup"
  - "set up research session"
  - "canonical state files"
  - "lineage mode"
version: 1.14.2.0
---

# Initialization

## 1. OVERVIEW

Sets up the deep research packet, canonical state files, and lineage mode before the loop begins.

Initialization is the workflow-owned entry into a research session. It creates the packet directory, seeds the state surfaces that the LEAF agent will read later, and decides whether the run is a fresh start, a resume, or a restart.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The live initialization contract classifies the packet state before it writes anything. It distinguishes fresh state, resumable state, completed-session state, and invalid state. Fresh runs create `research/deep-research-config.json`, the first JSONL line in `research/deep-research-state.jsonl`, `research/deep-research-strategy.md`, and `research/findings-registry.json`. Confirm mode adds a charter review gate before the first iteration continues.

Lineage handling is narrower than some older drafts. The runtime supports `new`, `resume`, `restart`, and `completed-continue`. Resume keeps the same session identifier and appends a typed `resumed` event. Restart archives the old research tree, mints a fresh session identifier, increments generation, and appends a typed `restarted` event. `completed-continue` is live through `--extend=N` on a completed run: it keeps the session identifier and generation, sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N, appends a typed `run_extended` event, and copies the prior synthesis to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration.

`fork` remains deferred and is not exposed as a live mode.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command | Defines the init phase inputs, packet outputs, and setup contract before the YAML workflow loads. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | Creates canonical state files on the initialization path. Confirm mode adds charter review and state checks. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` | Reference | Documents session classification, canonical names, and the resumed or restarted lifecycle event contract. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/deep-research-config.json` | Asset | Supplies the default config shape written during initialization. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/deep-research-strategy.md` | Asset | Supplies the initial strategy structure and anchor layout. |

# Feature Specification: Restore the research leaf fields the state-log projection drops (D7, D9)

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 1 -->
---

<!-- ANCHOR:metadata -->
## 1. METADATA

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Level** | 1 |
| **Priority** | P1 |
| **Status** | Planned. Operator decision recorded in `decision-record.md` (option 1, 2026-11-17) |
| **Created** | 2026-11-17 |
| **Branch** | `main` |
| **Parent Spec** | `../spec.md` |
| **Predecessor** | None by contract. Build after `../001-completed-run-extension/`, which edits the same four ledger files. The `../013-no-redispatch-landed-iteration/` open question was decided on 2026-11-17 (see Risks). |
| **Successor** | `../010-research-frontier-mode/spec.md` (the research frontier needs this slice first) |
| **Source** | Parent `research/research.md` §4 item 7, §12 item 6, §13 rows D7 and D9, §15, §20 Slice F |
<!-- /ANCHOR:metadata -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:problem -->
## 2. PROBLEM & PURPOSE

### Problem Statement
The research gateway upcast at `legacy-compatibility.ts:643-660` builds `iteration_completed` data from `status`, `newInfoRatio`, `ruledOut`, `focus` and `answeredQuestions` only, so every other leaf field is dropped before the state log is projected. Two checks starve as a result: the research `findings_not_enumerated` gate in `verify-iteration.cjs:533-535` reads `findingsCount` from the projected row, gets `NaN` and never runs, and the `focusAlignment` guard in `convergence.cjs:871-885` needs both `answeredQuestions` and `keyQuestions` on a record, so it always reports `notEvaluated`.

### Purpose
With the ledger upcast widened to carry the leaf fields, the research findings gate and the `focusAlignment` guard both run on real data, a run can recover from one off-focus iteration, and every changed default behavior is stated in the changelog.
<!-- /ANCHOR:problem -->

<!-- ANCHOR:measured-behavior -->
### Measured Behavior
Run from the parent packet's `research/` folder against generation 2, iteration 1.
```text
$ jq -c 'select(.type=="iteration") | keys' deltas/iter-001.jsonl
["agent_definition_loaded","answeredQuestions","edgeCase","findingsCount","focus","focusTrack","iteration","keyQuestions","lineageId","mode","newInfoRatio","noveltyJustification","resolved_route","ruledOut","run","runId","sessionId","sourcesQueried","status","target_agent","toolsUsed","type"]
$ jq -c 'select(.type=="iteration" and .iteration==1) | keys' deep-research-state.jsonl
["answeredQuestions","focus","iteration","mode","newInfoRatio","ruledOut","run","status","timestamp","type"]

---
title: "system-deep-loop"
description: "The advisor-routable hub for the active deep-loop workflow families over a nested runtime/ layer."
trigger_phrases:
  - "system deep loop"
  - "deep loop workflows"
  - "deep research review"
  - "deep-loop hub routing"
version: 1.6.11.0
---

# system-deep-loop

> Run iterative research or an iterative code review as a loop that keeps its state on disk and stops on a convergence signal.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### At a Glance

| Aspect | What it delivers |
|---|---|
| **Use it for** | Multi-round research or a multi-pass review. Each round builds on what the round before it found |
| **Invoke with** | `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint. A `/deep:*` command such as `/deep:research` or `/deep:review` also reaches it |
| **Works on** | A research topic with web or code sources. A review target such as a spec folder or a file set |
| **Produces** | The selected mode's artifacts and convergence state under the target spec folder, such as `research/research.md` or `review/review-report.md` |

### What It Does

`system-deep-loop` is one routing hub for two active loops. A request for iterative investigation goes to the `deep-research/` packet, which writes `research/research.md` under the target spec folder. A request for an iterative audit or a release-readiness verdict goes to `deep-review/`, which writes `review/review-report.md` and returns a verdict. Each packet owns its convergence math, state files, artifacts and tool guards. The shared `runtime/` beneath both modes holds executor config, prompt-pack rendering, atomic state and the coverage graph.

One-shot retrieval belongs to `@context`. Implementation planning belongs to `/speckit:plan`. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`.

### Why This Skill Exists

A deep-loop request can mean outward investigation or inward audit. The two loops share the same `runtime/` and the same append-only state path. One hub gives the family a single entry point, so the same request reaches the loop that owns it without a guess. The two packets still keep their own contracts, so one entry does not flatten the modes into one.

---

## 2. QUICK START

**Step 1: Invoke it.** Run `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint such as `research: <request>`. You can also call `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. Every surface reaches the same packet.

**Step 2: Check where a request routes.**

```bash
node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "deep review of the checklist changes"
```

Expected output: one JSON decision with `"action":"route"` and a target where `"workflowMode":"review"`. Exit code 0. Swap the prompt to `deep research on the routing drift` to see the research route.

**Step 3: Run the mode.** Pass the request to `/deep:review:auto "skill deep-review"`. The command workflow initializes `{spec_folder}/review/`, dispatches one review iteration per pass and synthesizes the report. The run produces `review-report.md` with a verdict, a findings registry, remediation workstreams and an audit appendix, plus the iteration files under `{spec_folder}/review/iterations/`.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

Routing runs in two stages. Stage one picks the mode: the hub scores the request against the vocabulary classes in `hub-router.json`. The winning `workflowMode` selects one entry in `mode-registry.json`. Stage two picks the leaves: `ROUTER.md` maps the request's intent to packet-qualified reference files. Each path resolves to a canonical pair in `leaf-manifest.json`. The packet then runs its loop. It initializes state, dispatches one fresh agent per iteration, checks convergence and synthesizes its artifact. The hub only routes, so per-mode logic stays in the packet.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  Request["Deep-loop request"] --> Hub["Hub scores the request"]
  Hub -->|research| Research["deep-research/ packet"]
  Hub -->|review| Review["deep-review/ packet"]
  Hub -->|no mode| Defer["defer: ask for the mode"]
  Research --> Runtime["runtime/"]
  Review --> Runtime
  Runtime --> Output["Artifacts and convergence state"]
```

### The Three-Tier Discriminator

Every registry entry carries three keys. `workflowMode` is the public mode key. Its values are `research` and `review`. `runtimeLoopType` is the convergence key that `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` consumes. The router reads it from the registry rather than inferring it from `workflowMode`. `backendKind` names the backend that runs the mode, `runtime-loop-type` for both active modes. A custom mode would declare an explicit `null` and name its own backend path.

### One Advisor Identity

Each packet carries no `graph-metadata.json`. The hub root carries the only one, so the advisor sees one skill identity no matter which surface reaches a mode.

---

# v4.1.0.0, Improved Chat Automation, Search & Session Handover

Long sessions now hand over cleanly, and Claude Code does it by default.

- Near its context limit, a session writes a handoff note and a fresh one picks up in the same terminal.
- The hop carries the running command, the goal and the session's name.
- Pi, OpenCode and Mistral Vibe now finish a hop too.

The skill advisor and the deep loops now say more and guess less.

- The advisor names the slash command behind a hub skill.
- It says whether the agent may run that command, start it or should ask first.
- Mistral Vibe joins as the eighth runtime, with Skilled's guards and advisor brief in every session.
- A deep review fails while a required check still fails, and a script makes that call.

Search, the spec commands and the overlay checks now report what really happened.

- Search answers say how much they left out.
- The spec commands save what they report and check their own work.
- Overlay checks in a vendored project refuse a bad write before it lands.

This release also changes what you install and what an agent may read.

- Skilled now needs a patched Node.js release.
- A new guard keeps the values in a `.env` file away from every agent, on all eight runtimes.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> Six changes can break an existing setup. Read these before you update.
>
> 1. Old session relaunch switch names are no longer read. [Old switch names](#c-old-switch-names-stop-working)
> 2. An open handoff note in the old state folder is not found. [Moving from session relaunch](#c-moving-from-session-relaunch)
> 3. The history lookup is no longer offered after a compaction. [Claimed note history](#c-a-claimed-note-offers-the-senders-history)
> 4. `/deep:ai-council` is gone, so run `/speckit:ai-council`. [AI Council](#c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent)
> 5. The standalone git pre-commit hook is gone. [Hook folders](#c-folders-say-what-they-guard)
> 6. Code Mode needs Node 24.22.1 or newer on 24. [Node.js](#c-patched-releases-are-required)

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

Several parts of Skilled said one thing and did another. Handover existed, but most people never had it on. Checks could pass while something underneath had failed. This release closes those gaps.

- Handover shipped off, so most Claude Code users never had it.
- A hop mid-command came back as a plain chat.
- The advisor named a skill, not the command to type.
- A deep review could pass while a required check failed.
- A search cut at its limit never said what it hid.
- An overlay re-pin could write a wrong value and still pass.
- Any agent could read `.env` and send its secrets to the model.
- Skilled still accepted Node 20 after an October security patch.

&nbsp;

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '/^## Deep Loop/,/^## /p' .skilled/changelog/skilled/v4.1.0.0.md | head -80
## Deep Loop

Deep loops run research and review passes on their own until their evidence says stop. Their verdicts and stop calls now come from scripts instead of judgment, so a rerun on the same state always agrees. The AI Council also moves out into an agent of its own.

#### The AI Council Is Its Own Agent <a id="c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent"></a>

> [!WARNING]
> `/deep:ai-council` is gone. Run `/speckit:ai-council` instead.

- It binds the spec folder and dispatches `@ai-council` once.
- The agent runs three rounds by default.
- It writes only under the packet's `ai-council/` folder.

#### A Review Fails While a Required Check Fails <a id="c-a-review-fails-while-a-required-check-fails"></a>

- A review returns FAIL when a P0 is open or a required check fails.
- The required checks are evidence, scope and coverage.
- Scripts compute the checks and the verdict.
- Coverage reads every shape a review records.
- The report carries the counts the verdict used.
- Open search debt makes a review CONDITIONAL.

#### Loops Stop the Same Way Every Time <a id="c-loops-stop-the-same-way-every-time"></a>

- `convergence.cjs --stop-decision` makes the stop call as one `stop_decision` object.
- The review vote follows your `convergenceThreshold`.
- A resume rebuilds its counts from the state log.
- Three failed passes in a row end the loop with reason `error`.
- `--restart` and `--dry-run` work on research and review.
- `maxDurationMinutes` ends a run on time.
- A review fan-out takes `--review-target`.
- Each deep command runs from one workflow file.

<details>
<summary><b>Smaller fixes</b></summary>

- Vibe runs bill your Vibe plan through your `vibe --setup` sign-in.
- A quota refusal counts as `quota`, not a crash.
- `fanout-run.cjs` refuses a one-lineage run.
- A pass with no recorded executor fails as `executor_missing`.
- Deep-loop scripts no longer hang on an open stdin.
- Deep-loop saves write continuity.
- One DeepSeek Harness lineage runs through `dispatch-iteration.cjs` with `--spec-folder`.
- A review that never ran says so.

</details>

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '200,265p' README.md
The skill advisor scores each prompt and puts a one-line routing brief in front of the agent. At a confidence of 0.8 or more, the agent must load that skill.

The advisor is a resident daemon behind one command-line front door. When the daemon is down, a local scorer answers instead and marks its answer stale. A setup or health prompt with no match gets a pointer to `/doctor:setup`. The [Skill Advisor README](.skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/README.md) has the scoring and the CLI.

### Spec Folders

Every conversation that changes files gets a numbered folder in the specs tree, grouped by track. The depth of its documents scales with the task. There are four documentation levels (1, 2, 3 and 3+). Level 1 holds a spec, a plan, a task list and an implementation summary. Level 2 adds acceptance criteria, and each row must be met, or settled in a decision record, before the packet can close. Level 3 adds architecture sections and a decision record. Level 3+ adds governance for complex work.

Beside those documents, each folder keeps two small metadata files that the save step refreshes and a scratch folder for temporary work. The validator runs 42 rules against a folder and reports what fails.

Folders nest in a fixed way. A track is the top folder, usually named after the skill or system the work touches. As a track grows, it can hold one level of named sub-tracks that group its packets by theme. Each sub-track numbers its own packets from 001 and keeps its own archive for finished work. A sub-track is always a proposal you approve.

A large feature becomes a phase parent instead. The parent keeps only its spec and the two metadata files, and its spec maps the phases. Each phase is a numbered child folder with its own plan, tasks and summary. A series parent works the same way for separate small changes to one artifact, such as one script or one command. It starts at the second change, so the history of that artifact stays in one place.

The [Spec Kit README](.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/README.md) shows the folder layout and covers the templates, the scripts and the validator.

### Continuity

The agent writes its progress into the spec folder it is working in. `/speckit:save` returns a save plan by default. Its full-auto mode writes the session summary, decisions and next steps. `/speckit:resume` reads them back in a fixed order: the handoff note, then the continuity block, then the spec docs.

Search stays plain on purpose. A committed trigger index matches the phrases authors declared, and ripgrep recipes cover free text. There is no database and no daemon on this path. A phrase nobody wrote returns a clean no-hit instead of a nearest guess.

After a compaction, a session can read back its own earlier turns with one read-only command. Your answers to the agent's questions are saved too, so the reason behind a choice lands in the packet's decision record.

### Handing Over A Long Session

A long session can hand its work to a fresh one before the context window fills. Near the limit, the session writes a handoff note and saves its spec folder. A small wrapper then starts the same runtime in the same terminal, and the new session claims the note. The running command, the goal and the session name carry over, and so does the model on every runtime except Vibe.

Handover is on by default for Claude Code. The [Session Handover](#session-handover) hook below shows how to turn it on for the others.

&nbsp;

## 5. 🔄 EVERYDAY WORKFLOWS

### You Just Ask

You never need to remember a command. Describe the task in plain words and the session handles the rest. On every prompt, the [skill advisor](#skill-advisor) reads the request and tells the agent which skill fits. When a workflow fits too, the brief names its command and how to run it: start it now, start it with its setup questions or offer it to you first.

The session automation covers the rest of the path. The [spec gate](#spec-gate) asks once which spec folder the work belongs to. The [session lifecycle](#session-lifecycle) hooks prime the context at start and save continuity at stop and compaction. Near the context limit, [session handover](#session-handover) carries the work into a fresh session.

The commands below are what runs underneath. Type one yourself only when you want a specific workflow or mode.

### Spec Kit Commands

`/speckit:complete` runs the whole path: intake, research, plan, build, verify and save. `/speckit:plan` stops after the plan, so someone can review it before any code is written. `/speckit:implement` builds from an existing plan. Each one accepts `:auto` to run without stopping or `:confirm` to pause at each step.

`/speckit:save` and `/speckit:resume` carry the work across sessions, as [Continuity](#continuity) describes. `/speckit:search` runs the trigger index and the ripgrep recipes over spec docs and skill docs. `/speckit:handoff` hands the work to a fresh session now, instead of waiting for the context limit.

### Deep Research And Deep Review

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open while a P0 is active or a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` adds N passes to a finished single-executor run in the same session. A review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

### CLI Orchestration

Your main CLI does not have to handle every task itself. The CLI orchestration hub hands a task to another AI CLI on the same machine and reads the result back. The agent you are talking to stays in charge. It writes the brief, waits for the answer and checks it before using it.

This lets you mix subscriptions. You can work in one CLI and send research, reviews or bulk work to another, billed to that CLI's own plan. The bridges are provider agnostic. Each CLI runs whatever providers and models you have set up in it. That is why Skilled supports eight runtimes: whichever plans you already pay for, one runtime is your main CLI and the others receive the work you hand off.

You ask for it in plain words, for example "use cli-opencode to review this diff". The advisor routes the request to the matching bridge. The bridge checks that its CLI is installed and that the dispatch passes its hard rules, then runs it. The deep loops use the same bridges, so one research or review run can spread its passes across several models. [Runtimes And CLI Bridges](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) lists all eight.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/feature-catalog.md:38:Initialization classifies the packet as fresh, resume, completed-session, or invalid-state before writing anything. It then creates canonical `deep-research-*` artifacts, seeds the reducer-owned findings registry, validates the research charter sections, and exposes `new`, `resume`, `restart`, and `completed-continue` (set by `--extend=N`) as runtime-supported lineage modes.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/feature-catalog.md:227:`deep-research-state.jsonl` stores the config record, iteration records, and typed events such as `resumed`, `restarted`, `blocked_stop`, `graph_convergence`, and `synthesis_complete`. Iteration lines can carry `ruledOut`, `noveltyJustification`, `convergenceSignals`, `focusTrack`, `sourceStrength`, and `graphEvents`, and the reader contract explicitly tolerates malformed lines by skipping them with defaults.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/initialization.md:19:Initialization is the workflow-owned entry into a research session. It creates the packet directory, seeds the state surfaces that the LEAF agent will read later, and decides whether the run is a fresh start, a resume, or a restart.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/initialization.md:27:Lineage handling is narrower than some older drafts. The runtime supports `new`, `resume`, `restart`, and `completed-continue`. Resume keeps the same session identifier and appends a typed `resumed` event. Restart archives the old research tree, mints a fresh session identifier, increments generation, and appends a typed `restarted` event. `completed-continue` is live through `--extend=N` on a completed run: it keeps the session identifier and generation, sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N, appends a typed `run_extended` event, and copies the prior synthesis to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/state-management/jsonl-state-log.md:25:The JSONL log begins with a config record, then appends one line per iteration plus typed event records such as `resumed`, `restarted`, `blocked_stop`, `graph_convergence`, `userPaused`, and `synthesis_complete`. Iteration rows can carry `newInfoRatio`, `noveltyJustification`, `convergenceSignals`, `ruledOut`, `focusTrack`, `sourceStrength`, and `graphEvents`, which makes the log the richest machine-readable packet surface.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.4.0.0.md:31:The packet contract now makes lineage fields explicit and shared. They are `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `run historyMode`, `generation` and `continuedFromRun`. It documents the `resume`, `restart`, `fork` and `completed-continue` lifecycle branches. They are the same across the skill.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.6.1.0.md:8:  - "resume and restart session events"
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.6.1.0.md:17:- **The two unsupported lifecycle modes are deferred.** When a research packet already existed, the workflow asked operators how to continue. The choices were `resume` the same run or start a fresh run. The other two were `fork` into a sibling run history and `completed-continue` to reopen a finished run after snapshotting its synthesis. The first two choices worked. `fork` and `completed-continue` had no workflow wiring. They did what `resume` did and quietly kept the old session id. So operators who thought they had branched a research run history overwrote the original. `SKILL.md`, `README.md`, both loop-protocol references and both command workflows now mark the modes as deferred. So do the Claude Code and Codex agent definitions. The normal happy path stays the same. The visible choices are `new`, `resume` and `restart`. The v1.5.0.0 changelog gained an append-only "Update (post-release)" block that points at this release.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.6.1.0.md:18:- **`resume` and `restart` record typed session events.** `resume` keeps the current session id. It appends a typed `resumed` event to the state log. `restart` archives the existing `research/` folder under a timestamped `research_archive/` directory. It mints a fresh session id. It appends a typed `restarted` event with the archive path. Both events carry the full run-history field set the reducer reads.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.6.1.0.md:24:No migration required. Existing research packets continue to resume exactly as before. Their state logs parse unchanged. The graph database is a dev-only derived cache. It rebuilds from iteration `graphEvents`. Operators relying on `fork` or `completed-continue` should archive the prior `research/` folder manually. Or they should choose `restart`, which does both in one step. Both deferred modes are reserved for a future release. They will return only with first-class event emission and replay coverage.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.6.2.0.md:18:- **Run history now shows resume and restart lineage.** The reducer detects lifecycle events. It updates run-history fields in the registry and the dashboard. It propagates lineage emitted to JSONL into config-visible state. The dashboard now surfaces `continuedFromRun`, which it never surfaced.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.6.2.0.md:22:- **The config template lists three modes.** `deep_research_config.json` removes `fork` and `completed-continue`. It changes `archiveRoot` to `research_archive`. It lists `new`, `resume` and `restart`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.5.0.0.md:60:This release advertised four lifecycle modes for re-entering a research session: `resume`, `restart`, `fork` and `completed-continue`. Configuration, JSONL, strategy and command docs described consistent run-history fields for all four. The v1.5.0.0 upgrade note said `resume` and `completed-continue` carried the v1.4.0.0 lineage contract. That contract was extended with legal-stop gate results and optional graph events.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/changelog/v1.5.0.0.md:62:On 2026-04-11, a post-release correction clarified that only `resume` and `restart` had workflow behavior. `fork` (start a sibling lineage) and `completed-continue` (reopen a finished run) had none. Selecting either silently did the same thing as `resume`.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:197:**Q: What is the difference between resume and restart?**
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:199:A: `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and appends a `resumed` event. `restart` archives the current `research/` tree under `research_archive/`, mints a fresh `sessionId`, increments `generation` and appends a `restarted` event. `completed-continue` comes from `--extend=N`: it reopens a completed run in the same session, raises the cap to the runs so far plus N, snapshots the prior synthesis and appends an `extended` event.

  lifecycleModes:
    - id: new

    - id: restart
      description: Start a new generation for the same target and archive the prior review packet state.
    - id: fork
      status: deferred
      description: Reserved compatibility branch. The current runtime does not emit or accept fork review lineages.
    - id: completed-continue
      description: Reopen a completed review in the same session and generation through --extend=N, after snapshotting the prior review report.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/feature-catalog/feature-catalog.md:220:A `tool.execute.before` hook resolves the real target agent (`orchestrate` always dispatches with `subagent_type: "general"`, so identity is parsed from `Agent: @X` / `Deep Route: ... target_agent=@X` prompt text) against `mode-registry.json` and compares it to any `mode=X` value declared in the dispatch prompt (Check 1). It also tracks per-session, per-target-agent dispatch counts for command-owned loop executors, exempting command-driven iterations, and flags non-command-driven repeats (Check 2). Default is mutate-and-warn for both; `SYSTEM_DEEP_LOOP_GUARD_REJECT=1` / `SYSTEM_DEEP_LOOP_GUARD_REJECT_LOOP=1` independently switch each check to fail-closed (throws, blocking the dispatch — confirmed live against the installed OpenCode host). Fails open on its own internal errors. A separate `event` hook, throttled to once per hour on `session.created`, sweeps/archives/prunes the plugin's own `.state/loop-guard` per-session files and rotates `guard-warnings.log`, mirroring `opencode-goal.js`'s retention pattern.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/feature-catalog/validation/mk-deep-loop-guard.md:43:Default behavior for both checks is mutate-and-warn: on a mismatch or loop-repeat, the plugin appends a `[system-deep-loop-guard] WARN: ...` line to `.skilled/skills/.state/loop-guard/guard-warnings.log` (never to stdout/stderr, which OpenCode's TUI paints onto the prompt input line during `tool.execute.before`) and lets the dispatch proceed. `SYSTEM_DEEP_LOOP_GUARD_REJECT=1` switches Check 1 to fail-closed; `SYSTEM_DEEP_LOOP_GUARD_REJECT_LOOP=1` independently switches Check 2 to fail-closed. Both throw-blocks-dispatch mechanisms are confirmed via live testing against the installed OpenCode host — throwing from `tool.execute.before` genuinely prevents the underlying tool call from executing, not merely logging a warning.

35:Coverage note: the playbook covers the hub's registry-driven routing at version `2.0.0.0`. It exercises:
65:4. `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/mode-registry.json` contains exactly 2 active modes in its `modes` array.

## 1. OVERVIEW

This playbook validates the `system-deep-loop` parent-skill hub through the operator-scenario set enumerated in Section 6 (Category Index) and Section 8 (Scenario Directory), which are the source of truth for the current scenario and category totals. Each scenario keeps its stable `{PREFIX}-NNN` ID in a dedicated per-scenario file whose YAML frontmatter carries the typed routing gold and whose body holds the full execution contract. A separate `compiled-routing/` directory carries one routing-gold bundle (`DL-CR-001`) outside this operator-scenario set; it is exempt from this playbook's contract and scored elsewhere.

Coverage note: the playbook covers the hub's registry-driven routing at version `2.0.0.0`. It exercises:
- Dominant-intent routing for the lexical modes: `research` and `review`.
- Explicit mode-hint override routing, such as `research: <request>`.
- Advisor integration: the single advisor identity `system-deep-loop`, lexical scoring, and no false fire on ordinary code-edit prompts.
- The three-tier discriminator: `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType`, and `backendKind`.
- State and convergence discipline: externalized state, artifact-root writes, convergence stop behavior, and hub logic boundaries.

### Realistic Test Model

1. A realistic user request is given to an orchestrator that has the `system-deep-loop` skill registered.
2. The orchestrator consults the skill advisor, command router, or explicit Skill invocation and decides whether to invoke the hub, a `/deep:*` command surface, another skill, or no deep-loop workflow.
3. The operator captures: which skill won the advisor vote, which `workflowMode` was selected, which command and agent surface would be used, which backend was resolved, which artifact root was named, and what the AI's response actually was.
4. The scenario passes only when the routing matches `mode-registry.json`, the source evidence is quoted or cited, and the user-visible outcome is sound for a real operator.

### What Each Feature File Should Explain

- The realistic user request that should trigger the behavior.
- The expected mode, command, agent, backend, and artifact root.
- Why that route is expected, quoting the matching `aliases`, `advisorRouting`, `command`, `agent`, `runtimeLoopType`, `backendKind`, or `artifactRoot` fields from `mode-registry.json`.
- The expected packet and whether it owns mode behavior or the hub only routes to it.
- The pass/fail criteria with binary grading.
- Failure triage steps.

---

## 2. GLOBAL PRECONDITIONS

1. Working directory is the repository root.
2. The hub skill is present at `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/`.
3. `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/SKILL.md` states that `mode-registry.json` is the single source of truth and that the hub holds no per-mode convergence, state, or synthesis logic.
4. `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/mode-registry.json` contains exactly 2 active modes in its `modes` array.
5. The skill advisor at `.skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/runtime/scripts/skill_advisor.py` is callable when advisor scenarios are executed.
6. The orchestrator runtime can invoke `Skill(system-deep-loop)` and can run `/deep:*` command prompts, or the operator can capture equivalent dry-run routing transcripts.
7. Operator evidence is written under `/tmp/dlw-<SCENARIO-ID>/` and never into project source paths unless a scenario explicitly states the mode's real artifact-root contract.
8. Multi-scenario waves cap at 5 parallel advisor probes. State-discipline checks that may create artifacts run serially.

## 2. LOOP LIFECYCLE

Use this catalog as the canonical inventory for the live `deep-review` feature surface. The numbered sections below group the system by lifecycle, packet state, review coverage, and finding controls so readers can move from a top-level summary into per-feature reference files without losing the contract behind each review phase.

|---|---:|---|

---

This catalog inventories the live `system-deep-loop` hub surface. The hub classifies a request into one of two `workflowMode` packets (`research`, `review`) through a three-tier discriminator, holding no per-mode logic itself. A default-on, flag-gated compiled-routing fast path can resolve the same decision ahead of this registry-driven classification without changing what it resolves to.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

Use this catalog as the current-state inventory for the `system-deep-loop` hub. The hub classifies intent and loads the resolved packet; the `/deep:*` commands and native agent types are complementary surfaces that reach the same packets through their own static routers.

---

## 2. DEEP-LOOP MODE ROUTING

### Registry-Driven Deep-Loop Mode Classification

#### Description

`mode-registry.json` is the single source of truth for the hub's two `workflowMode` packets, each carrying a `workflowMode`/`runtimeLoopType`/`backendKind` three-tier discriminator.

#### Current Reality

`runtimeLoopType` is the graph-backed convergence key consumed by `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`; `backendKind` is `runtime-loop-type` (research/review). A second-layer surface router at the root `ROUTER.md` supports router-replay benchmarking without re-deciding the mode.

- A fan-out run deals the seeded depth-1 nodes round-robin across lineages and merges them back under a partition guard, so no dealt node goes unreported.

### Invocation Contract

This skill is invoked exclusively through `/deep:research:auto` or `/deep:research:confirm` -- the command YAML owns state, dispatch, convergence, and synthesis. Never simulate the loop with ad hoc shell dispatch, nested CLI loops, direct `@deep-research` Task dispatch, `/tmp` prompt files, or state outside the resolved local research packet.

### Executor Selection Contract

The YAML workflow owns executor selection: native `@deep-research` by default, or a routed CLI executor. Never use ad hoc shell loops.
Cross-CLI delegation inside an executor sandbox is possible but discouraged.
Do not invoke the same CLI from within itself, and do not assume auth propagates to child CLIs.

The executor kinds are the `EXECUTOR_KINDS` array in `runtime/lib/deep-loop/executor-config.ts`.
The research YAML carries an inline branch for every kind. `native` dispatches the `@deep-research` agent.
`cli-codex`, `cli-claude-code`, `cli-opencode`, `cli-cursor`, `cli-devin`, `cli-pi` and `cli-deepseek-harness` each run one iteration through `runtime/scripts/dispatch-iteration.cjs`, and `cli-deepseek-harness` also passes `--spec-folder`.
`cli-mistral-vibe` runs only as a fan-out lineage, so its inline branch fails closed.
Flag compatibility remains in [loop-protocol.md §3](references/protocol/loop-protocol.md).

Executor invariants:

1. Produce a non-empty iteration markdown file at `{state_paths.iteration_pattern}`.
2. Record ONE JSON event record (a single object) through the append gateway (`runtime/scripts/append-mode-event.cjs --mode research --run-directory <spec folder> --event-json <file>`) with required fields: `type`, `iteration`, `newInfoRatio`, `status` and `focus`.
   The gateway takes a single event object per invocation, not a multi-line JSONL delta.
   The multi-line `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` file is a separate reducer-owned artifact.
   The gateway authorizes, fences, and receipts the write, then refreshes `{state_paths.state_log}` from the ledger. Do not write that file directly.
3. Respect the LEAF-agent constraint: no sub-dispatch, no nested loops, and no more tool calls per iteration than `config.maxToolCallsPerIteration`.

Failure modes include `iteration_file_missing`, `iteration_file_empty`, `jsonl_not_appended`, `jsonl_missing_fields`, `jsonl_parse_error` and `executor_missing`, which fails a non-native iteration whose state log or lineage metadata names no executor. Three trailing failed iterations stop the loop with stop reason `error`.

The veto never creates a stop of its own and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the `minIterations` floor. function stopDecisionConfig(config, loopType, args) {
  const antiConvergence = isPlainObject(config.antiConvergence) ? config.antiConvergence : {};
  const maxIterations = finiteNumberOrNull(config.maxIterations);
  const declaredMinIterations = finiteNumberOrNull(config.minIterations)
    ?? finiteNumberOrNull(antiConvergence.minIterations)
    ?? 0;
  const modeFlagGiven = args.convergenceMode !== undefined || args.convergence_mode !== undefined;
  const configMode = antiConvergence.convergenceMode;
  const depthModeValue = args.depthMode ?? args.depth_mode ?? antiConvergence.depthMode;
  return {
    maxIterations,
    // A floor above the cap could never be reached; cap it at the cap.
    minIterations: maxIterations === null
      ? declaredMinIterations
      : Math.min(declaredMinIterations, maxIterations),
    convergenceThreshold: finiteNumberOrNull(config.convergenceThreshold),
    stopPolicy: config.stopPolicy === 'max-iterations' ? 'max-iterations' : 'convergence',
    stuckThreshold: finiteNumberOrNull(config.stuckThreshold) ?? (loopType === 'review' ? 2 : 3),
    convergenceMode: modeFlagGiven
      ? readConvergenceModeConfig(args).mode
      : typeof configMode === 'string' && configMode !== '' ? configMode : 'default',

battery=json.load(open(base+'battery.json'))
filecov=set()
packetroots=[]
for r in battery['rows']:
    subj=r.get('subject')
    if not subj: continue
    if r['id'].startswith('S2-validate-document'):
        filecov.add(subj)
    if r['id'].startswith('S14-packet-validate'):
        packetroots.append(subj.rstrip('/')+'/')
def packet_cover(f):
    return any(f.startswith(p) for p in packetroots)
reviewed=set(A)|set(B)|set(D)
untouched=[]
for f in sorted(scope):
    if f in reviewed: continue
    if f in filecov: continue
    if packet_cover(f): continue
    untouched.append(f)
print('untouched',len(untouched))
for f in untouched: print(f)

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md:16:`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A run without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` turns on a research frontier: the run seeds depth-one nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as nodes under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps, and holds a convergence stop while frontier nodes stay open. Two live research pilots ran on one topic, and both failed the criterion that a stop candidate forms before the iteration cap: each run stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.

Each iteration's leaf `frontier_proposal` rows go through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs`, which admits each proposal into the node store. It applies the depth cap, the fan-out cap, the iteration budget, dedupe and cycle checks, and records a reason for each prune.

A review without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under the same caps as research, keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and holds open nodes as `searchDebt`. One live review pilot ran. ## 4. INTEGRATION & NAVIGATION

### When To Use This Skill

Reach for the hub when a request needs more than one round. Use the research mode for a topic that spans several sources, where each round should shape the next query. Use the review mode for a multi-pass audit of a spec folder or a file set. Use it for a release-readiness verdict. Both modes fit an unattended run, because each keeps its state on disk and checks convergence after every pass.

Use `@context` for a one-shot lookup and `/speckit:plan` for implementation planning. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`. A planning council is a separate agent, reached through `/speckit:ai-council`. It is not a mode of this hub.

### Related Skills

| Skill | Relationship |
|---|---|
| `system-spec-kit` | Owns the spec packets, validation and memory continuity that both modes read and write. |
| `sk-code` | Its code-review mode handles a single-pass review. The `deep-review/` packet runs the multi-pass loop. |

---

## 5. TROUBLESHOOTING

| What you see | Why | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| The compiled route returns `"action":"defer"` | The prompt names no mode, because `routerPolicy.defaultMode` is `null` | Add a mode word such as `deep research` or `deep review`. A mode hint works too. |
| The loop did not start | The mode was dispatched outside its command, so no workflow owned the state | Run `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. The command YAML owns state, dispatch and convergence. |
| The drift check reports `registry packet absent from hub index` | This README no longer names a packet folder that `mode-registry.json` registers | Add the folder name with a trailing slash to the README |

---

## 6. VERIFICATION

The parent-skill check validates the registry shape, the tool-surface union, the hub-router consistency and the ROUTER contract in one pass.

```bash
node .skilled/commands/doctor/scripts/parent-skill-check.cjs .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop
```

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:34:The deep review loop uses 9 state files under the resolved `{artifact_dir}/` owned by the target spec, and a run started with `--depth-mode=expansive` adds a tenth, `frontier-nodes.jsonl`:
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:47:| `frontier-nodes.jsonl` | JSON Lines | Append-only, expansive depth only (see Frontier Node Store) |
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:300:`review/frontier-nodes.jsonl` is the node-status store for a run started with `--depth-mode=expansive`; a default run never creates it. Rows are append-only and carry `{nodeId, parentId, depth, scope, question, status, reason, iteration, dedupeKey}`. The last row per `nodeId` wins, so a status change is one more row rather than an edit, and `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs --target review` is its only writer, for seed, admit and close.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:302:The reducer projects the store as `registry.frontier`: `nodes`, each with its `coveredDimensions`, `nextDimension` and `visits`; `stats`; `budget`; `activeNodeId`; and `activeNextDimension`. A node is `answered` once every configured dimension is covered for it. An iteration counts toward a node by its `frontierNodeId`, by the `Frontier node <id>` focus marker anywhere in the focus, or by the store's `active` row for that iteration.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:304:An open node is a `searchDebt` entry with `bugClass: frontier:<id>`, since the depth it stands for is unspent. An answered node becomes `candidateCoverage` `covered`; a pruned or abstained node becomes `ruledOut`; an exhausted or blocked node stays frontier-only, because filing it as debt would report work the run may no longer do.

#### See the frontier on the dashboard <a id="c-see-the-frontier-on-the-dashboard"></a>

- An expansive review adds a `## 10B. FRONTIER` section to its dashboard.
- The section shows frontier size, pruned sub-targets, revisits and budget spent at each depth.
- A default dashboard has no such section.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:144:**Convergence window.** Under `stopPolicy: convergence`, an extension with nothing new to cover re-stops on `all_questions_answered` at the first convergence check. The extension step prints a warning when this is likely; to keep the run going, pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`, or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:146:**Consecutive failures.** A run that completed on consecutive failures re-stops at the consecutive-failure check even under `--convergence-mode=off`, because that check runs before the `off` branch and the trailing failed iterations are still in the log.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:249:The active cache is capped at 100 entries. Existing `ideaRejected`, `ideaRejectedRemoved` and `ideaRejectedReset` replay rows are legacy aliases and remain accepted for backward compatibility.

# =============================================================================
# .env.example  —  Environment variable template
# =============================================================================
#
# CONVENTIONS
#   - Every variable here is OPTIONAL except the Code Mode provider secrets in
#     Section 1. The system runs on sensible defaults when nothing is set.
#   - Non-secret lines are COMMENTED and show the DEFAULT value. Uncomment the
#     line and change the value to override that default.
#   - Flag semantics:
#       Graduated ON  = enabled by default; set  false  to DISABLE.
#       Opt-in OFF    = disabled by default; set  true   to ENABLE.
#   - A default shown as empty means "unset by default":  # VAR=    # ...
#   - Booleans accept  1/true/yes/on  (and their negatives) unless noted.
#
# WHERE THESE ARE READ
#   Code Mode loads a filled-in .env for its provider secrets (Section 1), and
#   nothing else in this repository loads it. Hooks, validators and git hooks
#   read the process environment. The validation off switches in Section 5, the
#   hook kill-switches in Section 8 and the Jev feature switches in Section 17

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:156:Any new P0 forces another iteration. The override sets `newFindingsRatio` to at least 0.50, so a fresh blocker cannot be averaged away. The shipped config adds `antiConvergence.minIterations` of 2 and a `stopPolicy` of `fail-closed`, and the runtime capability loader rejects a missing or permissive stop policy. The floor holds a review run at `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` until its iteration count reaches `minIterations`, even when every dimension is clean.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:45:The four canonical dimensions are configured by default. Every configured dimension is required for coverage, so the loop can stop clean only after each one has been audited. A custom dimension you configure, such as `performance`, is reviewed after the canonical four. Correctness and security are the two the mode contract marks as required for severity coverage.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:141:The lineage block carries `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `lineageMode`, `generation`, `continuedFromRun` and `releaseReadinessState`. A first run is mode `new`. `resume` continues the same lineage without changing generation. `restart` opens a new generation and archives the prior packet. `completed-continue` is set by `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation and records `continuedFromRun`.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:156:Any new P0 forces another iteration. The override sets `newFindingsRatio` to at least 0.50, so a fresh blocker cannot be averaged away. The shipped config adds `antiConvergence.minIterations` of 2 and a `stopPolicy` of `fail-closed`, and the runtime capability loader rejects a missing or permissive stop policy. The floor holds a review run at `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` until its iteration count reaches `minIterations`, even when every dimension is clean.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`.

---
title: "Custom review dimensions"
description: "A review run accepts configured custom dimensions after the four canonical ones, tracks them in dimension coverage, and lets a covered custom dimension reach the all-dimensions-clean stop."
trigger_phrases:
  - "custom review dimension"
  - "custom dimension review"
  - "configured review dimension"
  - "review dimension coverage custom"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Custom review dimensions

## 1. OVERVIEW

A review normally audits four canonical dimensions: correctness, security, traceability and maintainability. A setup can name more. A configured name outside the canonical four is a custom dimension. It is tracked and covered like the others, ordered after the canonical four, and judged by its own name. It is not required for severity coverage.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Setup And Ordering

`.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` accepts custom names at setup, normalizes them, and orders them after the canonical four in the risk order. The review registry seeds the four canonical keys plus the custom keys.

### Coverage Tracking

`runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` derives each finding's dimension by matching it against the configured dimension universe, so a custom name that appears in the finding's focus is kept as its own dimension. When nothing matches, the derivation falls back to the iteration's first addressed dimension and then to `correctness`. `buildDimensionCoverage` builds coverage from the iteration records and the strategy dimensions.

### Review Contract And Agent

`deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` carries a `customDimensionRule`: priority after the canonical four, configured order, judged by its own name under the shared P0/P1/P2 severity doctrine, and `requiredForSeverityCoverage: false`. The review agent definition allows a configured custom dimension under the same rubric rule.

### Stop Behavior

A covered custom dimension counts toward the all-dimensions-clean stop, so a review with every configured dimension covered and clean can reach that stop.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Accepts, normalizes and orders custom names, and seeds the registry keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Derives each finding's dimension against the configured universe and builds dimension coverage. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | The `customDimensionRule` block. |
| `.skilled/agents/deep-review.md` | Agent contract | Allows a configured custom dimension under the custom rubric rule. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The custom dimension stop-path case. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-state-reducer.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Dimension coverage with custom names. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/custom-dimension-setup-and-universe.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-074: a configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/review-depth-v2-rollout/stop-gate-custom-dimension-coverage.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-075: a covered custom dimension reaches the all-dimensions-clean stop. |

---

    );
  }

  if (consecutiveFailureCount >= CONSECUTIVE_FAILURE_LIMIT) {
    return emit('STOP', 'error', 'consecutiveFailures', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  if (stuckCount >= stuckThreshold) {
    return emit('STUCK_RECOVERY', 'stuckRecovery', 'stuckThresholdReached', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  if (config.convergenceMode === 'off') {
    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'convergenceModeOff', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  if (config.stopPolicy === 'max-iterations') {
    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'stopPolicyMaxIterations', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  let candidate = null;
  if (loopType === 'review') {
    if (cleanReviewStop) {
      candidate = { reasonDetail: 'all_dimensions_clean', hardStop: true };
    } else if (signalResult.stopCandidate) {
      candidate = { reasonDetail: 'composite_converged', hardStop: false };
    }
  } else if (coverage.total > 0 && coverage.answered >= coverage.total) {
    candidate = { reasonDetail: 'all_questions_answered', hardStop: false };
  } else if (signalResult.stopCandidate) {
    candidate = { reasonDetail: 'composite_converged', hardStop: false };
  }

  if (!candidate) {
    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'noStopCandidate', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  if (minIterations > 1 && iterationCount < minIterations) {
    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'minIterationsNotReached', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  // An expansive run that still holds open frontier nodes turns a stop
  // candidate into another iteration. It never creates a stop, and everything
  // above it (cap, failures, stuck recovery, off, max-iterations, the floor)
  // still wins. A review clean candidate is a hard stop, so keying the veto on
  // soft candidates alone would leave a review run no way to spend the
  // iteration its open nodes need.
  const depthFacts = loopType === 'review' ? review : research;
  const vetoable = loopType === 'review' || !candidate.hardStop;
  if (config.depthMode === 'expansive'
    && vetoable
    && Number.isFinite(depthFacts.frontierOpen)
    && depthFacts.frontierOpen > 0) {
    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'frontierOpen', gatesAt(false), []);
  }

  const evaluated = gatesAt(candidate.hardStop);
  if (loopType === 'review' && evaluated.failed.length > 0) {
    return emit('BLOCKED', 'blockedStop', evaluated.failed[0], evaluated, evaluated.failed);
  }
  if (loopType !== 'review' && failedGuards.length > 0) {
    return emit('CONTINUE', null, 'qualityGuardFailed', evaluated, failedGuards);
  }

  const combined = combineGraphDecision(candidate, source.graphDecision, source.graphBlockers);
  return emit(
    combined.decision,
    combined.stopReason,
    combined.reasonDetail,
    evaluated,
    combined.blockedBy,
  );
}

// The stop decision reads loop state from files, so these helpers stay
// independent of the coverage graph and remain usable when the graph fails.
function isPlainObject(value) {
  return Boolean(value) && typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value);

#### How It Works

`deep-research-state.jsonl` stores the config record, iteration records, and typed events such as `resumed`, `restarted`, `blocked_stop`, `graph_convergence`, and `synthesis_complete`. Iteration lines can carry `ruledOut`, `noveltyJustification`, `convergenceSignals`, `focusTrack`, `sourceStrength`, and `graphEvents`, and the reader contract explicitly tolerates malformed lines by skipping them with defaults.

    - id: lifecycle-parity
      description: "Ensure lifecycle mode docs and workflow branches agree on the three supported modes: new, resume, and restart."
